  → both applied at adoption. The box is written BEFORE `transit_body`, which is
  the point: the transit seam is the one authority that re-resolves a pose
  against the world (ADR 0024), so this is a body arriving correctly sized rather
  than a live resize that could leave it inside the floor it was standing on.
  → `an_adopted_seat_gets_the_same_body_facts_as_a_spawned_one`, red-probed.
  ⚠ narrowed as they asked: this is the SEATING consumer. Prepared vitals still
  reach nothing on the ordinary worn-player path, and that stays open.
- ✔ **AA4a (their #4, HIGH) — an authored SPECIAL derived no special move**, which
  is H2's defect one field over and I wrote the comment that hid it. Both
  derivations passed a hard-coded `None` for special, reasoning that an authored
  persona puts its special into its authored MOVES. True only when it authored a
  moveset, and nothing requires one — so a character could carry
  `action_set.special = Some(..)` and advertise a signature move with no timeline,
  which is exactly what `ActionSet::special`'s own doc forbids.
  → folded in both places. It cannot double-fire: the derivation runs only when
  there is no authored moveset, so there is no second declaration to collide
  with.
  → `an_authored_special_action_set_derives_a_special_move`, red-probed.
- ✔ **AA4b (their #4, HIGH) — the contradiction the plan said did not exist.** I
  wrote, one commit earlier, that preparation had no contradictory authored
  HostCode configuration to reject and that a validator would be a test of
  itself. There is one: `PreparedKit::HostCode { authored_moveset }` exists so a
  character can take the host kit's capabilities and bring its own timelines, and
  if that moveset declares the `ranged` verb the press is owned twice — by the
  charge-projectile path the host kit installs and by the moveset verb. The exact
  double-ownership `RangedExecution::HostCharge` exists to prevent, through the
  one door it does not watch.
  → reported where `HostCode` is CHOSEN, not in `prepare_character`'s binding
  ledger, because deciding it needs the catalog and the catalog is deliberately
  out of scope until finalization (the Phase A split). Same shape and same reason
  as the malformed-Authored-row report directly above it.
- ⊘ **AA4c (their #4) — the control gate is NOT a second authority.** Argued, not
  accepted. `gate_worn_player_control` does ask
  `CharacterCatalog::playable_kit_source` rather than reading `RangedExecution`,
  and its comment says why: Commands are deferred, so consulting the identity
  prevents a one-tick projectile leak on an Authored re-wear. The review's
  concern is that a registered-only character is classified as unknown there. It
  is — and the gate blanks the press unless the catalog allows it **AND** the
  body carries the charge marker:
  `if !allows_charge_projectiles || !has_charge_marker`. For a registered-only
  authored persona the marker is absent, so the press is blanked. Both must
  agree to permit; either alone denies. That is a belt-and-braces gate, not a
  competing resolver, and the failure mode it is accused of does not exist.
  ⚠ recorded rather than silently skipped: the next reviewer will see the same
  shape and should see the argument with it.
- ▢ **AA2 (their #2, HIGH) — `ActiveMatch` is simulation-critical and NOT rollback
  state.** Accepted in full and deliberately not fixed here. It gates two
  behaviours inside the sim — seating stops the moment it exists, and the
  countdown advances only when it does — it holds live `Entity` values, it is
  mutated from ordinary `Update` by topology reconciliation, and only
  `VersusMatch` is rollback-registered. A rewind across the activation tick
  returns the fighters to a pre-activation frame while the resource stays, so
  seating returns early believing the match is live and the countdown advances
  against stale entities.
  ⛔ this is a REAL blocker and not a small one: snapshotting raw `Entity` values
  needs correct remapping and does not touch the `Update`-mutation half. The
  review's own framing is right — either activation completes before the rollback
  session begins, or the selection becomes rollback-owned and participant
  references are reconstructed deterministically. Both are design work, and doing
  either badly is worse than the current state, which is why this row is written
  and not coded.
  → the same row also means Y′9's stamp repair runs in `Update` on a resource a
  rollback does not own. The repair is idempotent and derives its value from the
  frozen topology, so it re-applies harmlessly after a rewind — but that is a
  property worth stating rather than discovering.
- ✔ **AA6 (their #6) — the route-camera grace was preempted by the generic
  timeout.** `fail_after_timeout` used a flat `frames > 600` while route
  readiness allows `warmup + 600`, so for ANY warmup above zero the generic
  backstop fired first: the route-specific diagnostic was unreachable and a
  `--warmup` above 600 could never complete. Both numbers are mine, from this
  session. The backstop is now derived from what the run was asked to wait for.
  ⚠ it never produced a false SUCCESS, which is why it survived a session of use.
  A backstop that fires before the thing it backstops is a shorter timeout
  wearing a policy's name.

## Y″. GPT-5.6 review of the Phase A/B work (2026-07-29) — H6 was not closed

Handed over mid-Phase-B. Every claim was checkable and every one I checked held.
The headline finding is that **I closed H6 on a probe that exercised the wrong
population**, which is the same class of error as the "7 of 7" closeout and worth
recording as such rather than as a bug.

- ✔ **Y″1 (their #1, HIGH) — H6 REOPENED and now genuinely closed.** The
  projection noticed a new cast generation, correctly skipped the kit for persona
  bodies (their kit belongs to `apply_worn_character_gameplay`), and then stamped
  `ProjectedCharacterKit` with the new generation ANYWAY. That writer runs only on
  `Changed<WornCharacter>` / `Changed<BodyAbilities>`, and a cast replacement
  changes neither — so the body kept the retired kit AND recorded that it was
  current, which is worse than a missed update because nothing would revisit it.
  ⛔ **my H6 probe spawned a body without the persona writer's columns**, so it
  took the projection's fallback branch. It proved generation replacement for the
  population the projection still owns while being cited as evidence for the one
  it does not. A probe that goes red for the wrong reason is still a probe that
  proves nothing.
  The fix: `apply_worn_character_gameplay` drops the change-detection filter and
  refreshes when the identity changed OR its own record is stale, then stamps
  `avatar::PersonaBaseline` — a SECOND marker, deliberately. Two writers sharing
  one "already done" record is how one certifies work the other never performed.
  One writer, one record, each stamped only after its own work lands.
- ✔ **Y″2 (their #2, HIGH) — retraction inferred history from the new registry.**
  It looked the previously-projected id up in the CURRENT registry and removed
  whatever THAT definition carried. Fails in the two cases that matter: a same-id
  replacement that stops authoring (lookup returns the new empty definition →
  nothing retracted, body keeps a retired hurtbox doc forever) and a character
  LEAVING the cast (lookup returns nothing). `ProjectedCharacterKit` records
  `granted_hurtboxes` / `granted_movement_tuning` at grant time now. Historical
  ownership is not a property of the new authority.
- ✔ **Y″3 (their #3, HIGH) — authored max health did not reach the ADOPTED seat.**
  A spawned seat took `prepared.vitals.max_health`; the adopted primary kept
  whatever its session established from the legacy catalog or the default player
  health. The versus duelists author 60 and 52 — a deliberate trade, one fighter
  paying for a faster smash — and it did not apply to seat 0. Adoption is the one
  place a body becomes a fighter, so it is where the authored maximum lands until
  match activation exists.
- ✔ **Y″7 (their #7) — the solo branch counted ENTITIES, not players.**
  `assign_local_seat_devices` decided solo-vs-multi from `seats.iter().len()`,
  which is an observation of activation progress. During activation a two-player
  topology can exist while only the primary participant entity does, and in that
  window the primary got any-pad behaviour — a controller meant for handle 1 could
  drive seat 0. It reads `topology.players()` when one is frozen.
- ✔ **Y″8 (their #8) — the stale comments are gone.** The disproven
  "`EnemyActorBundle` carries neither" diagnosis survived in a dead comment block
  in `presentation.rs` *and* in two planning docs, alongside the correction, in
  the same files. Also corrected: `SyncTestSettings::default` no longer claims the
  shipped host installs one player, `LocalSeatTopology` documents its GAMEPLAY-
  session lifetime rather than "starts or rebases", and the registry no longer
  describes itself as mutated in place by registration.

### Open, with the evidence measured rather than asserted

- ✔ **Y″4 — `Vitals.mass` was DISCONNECTED, not dead. CLOSED.** The first pass said "zero
  runtime reads" and stopped there, which was true about the field and wrong about
  the concept.
  `ambition_actors::features::Mass(pub f32)` exists, is rollback-registered as
  `mount.mass`, and has exactly ONE consumer: the mount pair's mass-weighted
  centre of gravity (ADR 0020) — a heavy mount keeps the COG near itself, so the
  lighter rider orbits it on a gravity flip. It is populated from the ROSTER
  archetype (`spec.mass`) and never from the character definition.
  So `Vitals.mass` is a SECOND declaration of a fact only the roster could state:
  the split-authority shape this campaign has spent two days removing, hiding as a
  dead field. A registered character that authors a mass should get one, exactly
  as it gets its authored `max_health`.
  ✔ **WIRED 2026-07-29.** Seated construction carries
  `Mass(prepared.vitals.mass)`, red-probed against the default. The field doc no
  longer claims the value is unconsumed.
- ✔ **Y″5 — CLOSED 2026-07-29. `BodySource` has consumers now, one per variant.**
  `SpriteAuthored { world_per_pixel }` becomes a `SpritePosedBody`, installed by
  the projection and retracted with its other grants (`granted_posed_body`, same
  reasoning as Y″2). `Explicit { half_extents }` is consumed by SEATING in place
  of the `SEAT_BODY_PX` placeholder.
  ⚠ **the split is the design, not a compromise.** A projection that resized a
  LIVE body would be a second geometry authority beside the transit seam (ADR
  0024); a spawn-time constant cannot express a silhouette that changes with the
  pose. Each variant goes to the authority that can honour it.
  ⚠ **what this really fixed:** `SpritePosedBody` was inserted from exactly ONE
  place in the repository — a bespoke app-side system in the Mary-O snake matching
  on a display name. So body geometry was still declared through a second seam,
  which is the problem `register_character` exists to delete. Any registered
  character can now declare a sprite-shaped body with no app-side code.
  → the snake's own tagging system should migrate onto this. Content, not
  architecture, and deliberately not in the same commit as the seam.

- ▢ **Y″5-old (superseded) — `BodySource` has no production consumer.** Neither variant has any
  effect: `Explicit` half-extents ignored, `SpriteAuthored.world_per_pixel`
  ignored, no construction path selects geometry from it. Seated fighters get the
  fixed `SEAT_BODY_PX` placeholder and are later resized by the sprite-pose
  pipeline — a DIFFERENT authority that happens to produce a plausible result. A
  provider can author an explicit body and get another size. Either consume it in
  complete body construction or delete it in favour of the sheet authority.
- ◐ **Y″6 — the prepared `portrait` has no runtime reader, and it is the SAME
  shape as `Vitals.mass` was.** Checked properly rather than assumed (2026-07-29):
  * the CONCEPT has a real consumer — `ambition_content::presentation::dialog`
    resolves a speaker portrait through `portrait_catalog` override, then
    `CharacterCatalog::portrait_ref`, then a clip out of the portrait registry;
  * the PREPARED field is a second declaration nothing reads.
  ⚠ **but it cannot be wired the way mass was, and the reason is the useful part.**
  `CharacterDefinition.portrait` declares a portrait TARGET (a name, resolved at
  preparation against the composition's vocabulary). `portrait_ref` yields
  `{ image, manifest, default_clip }` — concrete paths. **There is no
  target → portrait-art resolver anywhere**, so the authored target has nothing to
  resolve THROUGH, which is why it reaches nothing.
  ▢ two honest options, and the choice is a product one: build the resolver (the
  sheet path already has exactly this shape — `SheetTarget` resolves a name to a
  manifest), or delete `CharacterDefinition.portrait` and let the catalog own
  portraits outright.
  ⛔ do NOT "wire it" by copying the catalog's paths onto the definition. That
  makes two places declaring the same concrete art, which is the split this whole
  campaign has been removing.

⚠ **Y″4/5/6 are the honest limit of "a prepared character has no questions left
in it".** The KIT has no questions left. Three authoring fields on the same
struct are decoration until something reads them, and saying so is cheaper than
the next reviewer finding it.

## Z′. Found by CAPTURING the versus stage and looking at it (2026-07-29)

The suite was 43/44 green and the stage was unfair. Both of these were visible in
the first screenshot and neither had a test, because nothing had thought to ask.

- ✔ **Z′1 — player one could FLY and TELEPORT in a versus match.** Seat 0 is the
  ADOPTED primary player and arrives carrying whatever the session granted it; in
  the shipped host that is the sandbox dev kit (blink, fly, shield). Every other
  seat is SPAWNED with the basic run-and-jump floor from `AncillaryMovementBundle`.
  The on-screen control legend advertised the difference — Blink / Fly Toggle /
  Shield sitting over the health bars — which is how it was spotted.
  ⚠ **writing `BodyAbilities` alone would have been undone.** The effective set is
  `AbilityBase ∩ editable_mask`, recomputed every frame for the primary player by
  the dev-tools sync, so the base has to move too. Read before it was tested.
  Declared by the ROSTER (`fighter_abilities`), like `opens_suspended`: what a
  fighter may do is a rule of the match, and a stage that wants asymmetric
  fighters says something else. Safe because the versus stage declares its own
  `StartingCharacter`, so that body belongs to the versus session.
- ✔ **Z′2 — `capture_scene --route` silently ate a positional.** Route mode takes
  no room id, so its FIRST positional is the output — and the room-mode form
  (`<ROOM> <FOCUS> <OUT.png> <WxH> --route ...`) adopted the room id as the output
  path, rendered the entire scene, and failed at the very end with "the image
  format could not be determined", naming nothing the caller typed. Positionals
  are CLASSIFIED now (size / image path / error), and an unrecognised one is named
  before any work happens. A verification tool whose failure mode is a confusing
  message at the end is a tool people stop running.

### Still open, and visible in the same screenshot

- ✔ **Z′3 — CLOSED, and it was not a HUD problem at all.** Jon looked at the
  screenshot and said the on-screen controls were in the wrong place. They were:
  `apply_touch_control_placement` implemented "no footprint published → controls
  hidden" as *collapse the node to a zero rect*. A zero-size node still lays out,
  and every child of these surfaces is `PositionType::Absolute` — so the joystick
  art, the U/D/L/R glyphs and the action labels kept drawing at the collapsed
  node's origin, the top-left corner of the screen. `mobile_touch` is in the
  default `desktop_dev` feature set, and in the versus capture the surfaces had no
  placement for all 150 warmup frames, so the cluster sat over the scoreboard.
  ⛔ **I first wrote "EVERY desktop run", and it is not supported.** The
  bottom-left test below shows a VISIBLE touch session places correctly, so the
  misdraw needs the surfaces UNPLACED — which means the overlay hidden. Both
  `TouchControlsVisible` and its user setting default to `true`, so I cannot claim
  the hidden state is the common one; I never checked it at runtime. The fix is
  unchanged and still right. A wrong severity is how a real row gets triaged for
  the wrong reason.
  `Display::None` now, which is what the old comment believed it was doing.
  ⚠ **an existing test asserted the bug.** `an_unplaced_surface_collapses` checked
  `width == 0 && height == 0` and passed throughout, because the size was never
  the problem. It asserts `display` now and is renamed for what it means.
  ⚠ **two of my own hypotheses were wrong before this one was right**, and both
  were discarded by instrumenting rather than by reasoning harder: it was not the
  HUD regions (reverted, on a branch) and it was not the missing primary window
  (`display_rect` was already sane; the resolver runs fine). Printing
  `ResolvedGameplayPresentation` took two minutes and ended the guessing.
  → the reserved-surround branch `wip/versus-reserved-surround` is now probably
  UNNECESSARY: the overlap it was meant to fix was this. Kept for Jon.
  ✔ **the residual is CLOSED, and it was the right thing to worry about.** Hiding
  an unplaced surface is only correct if a surface that SHOULD be on screen still
  gets a rectangle — otherwise the repair for "the d-pad drew in the wrong
  corner" is "the d-pad does not draw", which is WORSE for someone holding a
  phone: a misplaced stick is playable, an absent one is not.
  `a_visible_touch_stick_is_placed_at_the_bottom_left_corner` drives the real
  resolver with the real footprints and then the real placement system, and the
  stick lands flush with the bottom-left of the safe area with `Display::Flex`.
  So the fix is inert for a touch session and only removes the art that had no
  business being drawn. Jon asked for exactly this test rather than an
  investigation, and it answered the question faster than the investigation would
  have.

- ▢ **Z′3-old (superseded) — the control legend overlaps the versus HUD.** "Long Guard 60/60" and
  "Close Guard 52/52" are drawn under the virtual d-pad and the `Interact (F)`
  label. Two overlays anchored to the same corner, neither aware of the other.
  ⚠ **an attempt was made and REVERTED, and the reason is worth more than the
  attempt.** The HUD slots ask for `SurroundRegion::Top`, and asking is all they
  can do — the renderer honours a region only when the active profile RESERVES a
  surround. So the obvious fix is to give the versus experience
  `fixed_aspect(16,9).with_reserved_surround()`. It changed nothing visible at
  16:9 (no slack to reserve, which is expected) AND nothing at 16:10 (which is
  not). Reverted rather than shipped: a change that claims to fix a visible bug
  and cannot be shown to is the thing this whole session has been arguing against.
  ⛔ **`capture_scene` CANNOT verify viewport-policy changes.** It adopts the
  route's cameras and retargets them at its own capture image, which overrides
  the viewport the profile would have chosen. So letterboxing, reserved surrounds
  and control placement are all invisible to it — the tool that found Z′1 is blind
  to this entire class. Either the capture honours the resolved viewport, or this
  class needs a different instrument; until then, do not use a screenshot as
  evidence about a profile.
  → `SurroundRegion` has only Left/Right/Top/Bottom, so "put the scoreboard in a
  corner the legend does not own" is not currently expressible either.
- ▢ **Z′4 — `Interact (F)` is offered on a fighting stage** where there is nothing
  to interact with.
  ⚠ **scoped 2026-07-29, and the obvious fix is the wrong one.** `derive_action_scheme`
  grants Interact unconditionally, and its comment says why: *"the actual prompt
  (Talk / Open / …) resolves against nearby interactables at press time"*. Under
  that design, advertising Interact with nothing nearby is not a versus bug — it
  is prompt noise on EVERY stage, and gating it for fighters would fix the symptom
  where it was noticed while leaving it everywhere else.
  The real question is whether the prompt should advertise a verb that currently
  resolves to nothing, which needs the prompt to know about nearby interactables —
  a bigger change than it looks, and a product call about how much a prompt should
  promise.
  ⚠ layering, for whoever takes it: `derive_action_scheme` lives in
  `ambition_characters`, below both production callers
  (`ambition_actors::action_scheme` and `ambition_sim_view::control_prompt`), so
  neither `MatchSeat` nor any match concept is visible to it. A per-body marker
  from `ambition_characters` is the only shape both callers can read — the same
  route `ScriptedControl` already takes.

- ✔ **Z′6 — CLOSED 2026-07-29. A surface is born hidden.**
  `placement.rs` already documents it: on the frame a joystick is created it shows
  "one visible frame at the joystick crate's own corner position, over gameplay,
  whatever the touch-controls setting says". `Display::None` does NOT cover it — a
  `Node`'s `display` defaults to `Flex`, so the surface renders once before
  `apply_touch_control_placement` has ever run on it.
  Same class as Z′3, strictly smaller symptom (one frame, not steady state), found
  while checking whether Z′3's severity claim was true. The fix is to spawn these
  surfaces hidden and let placement reveal them, rather than spawn them visible
  and hide them a frame later.
  Done with an OBSERVER on `Add<TouchSurface>` rather than a `display` field on
  each spawned `Node`, because the movement surface is not spawned by this crate
  at all — it is a `TouchSurface` inserted onto an entity the joystick crate owns,
  which is precisely the one the symptom names. The test asserts the state
  WITHOUT running the placement system, since the whole point is the window
  between creation and the first placement pass.

- ✔ **Z′7 — CLOSED 2026-07-29. `MatchSeated` said seating FINISHED, never WHO.** So nothing can ask
  whether the live fighters are still the set the match was built from, which is
  exactly why Y′9's post-seating disagreement can only be REPORTED. Replace it
  with an `ActiveMatch` naming the seated bodies and the topology generation they
  were activated against, published in ONE insert on the tick every participant
  has a body. That also removes the countdown's `None`-means-go special case:
  absent would genuinely mean no match is live.
  Designed in full; not started. It is the last piece of "match activation as an
  atomic transaction", which both GPT-5.6 reviews and
  [character-preparation-finalization-plan.md](character-preparation-finalization-plan.md)
  name as the next step.
  ✔ `ActiveMatch { participants, seat_topology }` replaces the bool. Published in
  ONE `insert_resource` on the tick the last seat fills, assembled from the seats
  filled on earlier ticks plus this pass's — seating retries, so the closing tick
  usually adds only the last body and a list built from that tick alone would be
  wrong.
  ✔ the countdown's `None`-means-go special case is GONE with the latch. Absent
  now genuinely means no match is live, so there is nothing to count into.
  ✔ the roster lifecycle removes the activation on route entry AND exit — a new
  roster is not yet a match, and an activation that outlives its match is the next
  game inheriting somebody else's fighters.
  ⚠ **the private fields are the point.** Production has exactly one publisher
  (seating); `ActiveMatch::for_test` is the named hatch for a fixture that needs a
  live match without standing seating up.
  → what this ENABLES and does not yet do: `reconcile_roster_with_frozen_topology`
  can now compare the activation's recorded topology against the live one and name
  the bodies involved. It still only warns. Repairing a live match is a design
  question (reseat? forfeit? refuse the join?) and belongs to whoever answers it,
  not to the commit that made it askable.

- ✔ **Z′8 — CLOSED 2026-07-29, and it was a CAPTURE artifact, not a game bug.**
  The announce card is declared `.centered()` and did not render centred. Captured after match activation landed: the countdown completes and
  "FIGHT" appears, which is the activation working end to end — but the card sits
  right of centre on a 960px capture (roughly x≈750-830, not ≈480) and lands on
  top of the right-hand fighter.
  It is the same family as Z′3: a HUD slot's declared intent and its drawn
  position disagreeing. Unlike Z′3 the cause is not known yet — `.centered()` may
  be centring within a region rather than the display, which would make it correct
  code with a surprising region.
  ⚠ a round-start card that covers a fighter is a genuine feel problem on the one
  stage a stranger is most likely to open, so this is polish with real stakes
  rather than a pixel nit.
  → **whoever takes this should check the drawn rect against the resolved HUD
  region first.** The instrument exists: two `eprintln!`s in `capture_scene`
  ended two rounds of wrong guessing about Z′3 in minutes, and reasoning about
  layout from the screenshot alone produced two wrong answers before that.

  ⛔ **ROOT CAUSE, and it invalidates a revert I made earlier today.** The capture
  runs `OffscreenGpu`, which sets `primary_window: None`. The host's layout
  resolver returns without a window, so `ResolvedGameplayPresentation` kept its
  DEFAULT — display rect `WINDOW_W x WINDOW_H`, 1600x900. Every HUD slot laid out
  for a 1600x900 screen and was rendered into a 960x540 image, so a card centred
  at x=800 landed right of centre. Measured, not reasoned: `display=1600x900`
  against `capture=960x540`, and 1600/2 = 800 ≈ where the card was.
  ⚠ **I had built exactly this fix (`HeadlessDisplaySurface`) and REVERTED it as
  unproven.** It was unproven because I tested it against the touch-overlay
  symptom, which was a different bug — and because the insert went into the
  ROOM-mode app builder while route captures build their own app through
  `build_visible_app`. Two builders, same trap as the `--route` positional bug.
  ✔ with the surface declared in BOTH builders the capture resolves 960x540, the
  FIGHT card is centred, and the touch controls appear where they belong: stick
  bottom-left, Jab/Jump bottom-right. **The capture now shows what a player sees**,
  which is what makes it evidence rather than decoration.
  → this also closes Z′5 (make `capture_scene` honour the resolved viewport) and
  retires `wip/versus-reserved-surround`: the HUD overlap that branch targeted was
  the unresolved layout, not a missing surround.

- ✔ **Z′9 — "centred" menu text was anchored at the centre and drawn to the right
  of it.** (2026-07-29, found by capturing the LAUNCHER — the first screen anybody
  sees.)
  A text node with no width shrinks to its content, so `left: Percent(50)` puts
  the node's LEFT EDGE at the middle; `Justify::Center` then centres the line
  inside a box exactly as wide as the line, which does nothing. Every centred
  heading and footer in the shell was drawn right of where it asked to be.
  On the launcher that put the game TITLE on top of the first menu row and ran
  "Arrow keys select · Enter launches" past the panel edge — at 1600x900, so not a
  scaling artifact.
  ⚠ **the HUD had already hit this and fixed it for its own cards.** The comment
  in `hud/declared.rs` describes the bug exactly — *"the card starts at centre and
  runs off to the right … which is exactly how this shipped"* — and the menu
  renderer had the identical defect, untouched. A fix that is not carried to the
  other renderer with the same shape is half a fix.
  A centred line SPANS its container now; right-aligned spans up to its anchor;
  only `Left` treats `x` as a left edge. Every `Center` caller in the shell passes
  x = 50, so "centre on the container" is what they all meant.

- ▢ **Z′10 — `wip/versus-reserved-surround` is testable again, and may now be
  right.** It was parked as unverifiable because the capture could not show a
  viewport policy. It can: the Mary-O route captures with visible 4:3 letterboxing
  and its SCORE/COINS/TIME/LIVES HUD drawn in the reserved surround, which is
  exactly the mechanism the branch declares for versus.
  So the branch is no longer a guess — someone can apply it, capture
  `versus_gameplay`, and SEE whether a reserved top band improves the scoreboard
  or wastes arena width. That is a judgement about the stage rather than about the
  code, which is why it stays on a branch.
  ⚠ note what it does NOT fix any more: the HUD/legend overlap it was written for
  turned out to be the unresolved layout plus the touch overlay drawing unplaced
  surfaces. Both are fixed on main.

- ✔ **Z′11 — Pocket drew a BLUE RECTANGLE where its character should be.**
  (2026-07-29, found by capturing every shipped route and looking at each.)
  Picking "Pocket" from the launcher showed a plain box standing on the platform.
  The engine's own ledger named it in one line once asked:
  `staged=["pocket_runner"] failures=[("pocket_runner", UnknownCharacter)]`.
  ⛔ **Pocket was the one provider that never migrated to the registration seam.**
  It declared a `CharacterCatalogFragment` and stopped. `declare_registered_characters`
  reads the PREPARED REGISTRY, so a catalog-only character is unknown to the art
  pipeline — the catalog says what a character IS, `register_character` is what
  makes it exist.
  ⛔ **and its catalog row named art that does not exist in this repository**:
  `sprites/mary_o_spritesheet.*`. Only `super_mary_o_spritesheet.*` is present, so
  even the legacy path had nothing to load. Two independent faults, one symptom.
  ⚠ **the art guard names Pocket in its own comment and could not see this.**
  `every_registered_character_resolves_the_art_it_declares` iterates the registry,
  and a provider that registers nothing passes by being ABSENT from the population
  it inspects. It now asserts each shipped provider's starting character IS in the
  registry — a guard over a population cannot notice something missing from the
  population, so the population has to be named.
  ⚠ **I lost this fix once to `git checkout -- <file>` on an unstaged edit**, which
  is the hazard already recorded in memory from a previous session. Stage before
  probing; `git restore --source=:0` reverts a probe without touching the work.

- ✔ **Z′12 — every capture I took showed a DEV build, so it overstated the noise.**
  `capture_scene` builds with the default `desktop_dev` features and default user
  settings, so each screenshot carries the "F1 debug | F3 inspector" banner, the
  FPS/frame overlay, and debug nameplates (`military_tower_door`,
  `hall_of_bosses_door`, "Solid Snake", "AI Slop"). A stranger running a player
  build sees none of that.
  This matters because the tool's stated purpose is showing what is actually on
  screen. Half of what is on screen in these captures is scaffolding, and I have
  been reading it as product — the door IDs in particular look like raw
  identifiers leaking into UI, and they are debug nameplates.
  ✔ **DONE:** `--dev-overlays`, off by default. The debug banner and the FPS
  counter are gone from a default capture, and "Jump (Z)" is legible instead of
  overlapping the frame-time readout.
  ⚠ **it had to be a SYSTEM, not a build-time insert.** Settings are loaded and
  re-applied during startup, so the first version wrote the flags before the first
  update and the load quietly overwrote them — every overlay still on screen, no
  error. Forcing them each frame is order-independent and costs nothing here.
  ⚠ written as SETTINGS rather than by disabling plugins, so a clean capture is a
  configuration a player can actually reach rather than a special build.
  ⚠ do not conclude from this that the dev captures were useless: the fly-in-versus
  kit, the corner d-pad, the launcher title and Pocket's blue rectangle were all
  product bugs found in dev-build screenshots. The point is only that the noise
  floor is higher than it looks, so absence of an obvious defect is weaker evidence
  than it appears.

- ✔ **AB0 — suite 22/22 twice.** 656s at `f10ee3898`, covering F0e's core-feel
  change and all six round-3 review findings; then 332s at `b7297131d`, covering
  the actor sheet demand, the ranged cancel window, and the possession
  assertion.
  ⚠ an earlier run of the same tree is DISCARDED, not counted: I ran `git stash`
  mid-run to check whether a warning was pre-existing, which briefly reverted the
  working tree under a compiling suite. It reported no failures and it does not
  matter — a result from a tree that changed underneath it is not evidence, and
  saying "it passed anyway" would be exactly the habit this ledger exists to
  break. The 22/22 above is a clean run started after the commit.

- ◐ **AB5 — the intro's COMBAT TUTORIAL is fought against red rectangles, and
  that is by design.** (2026-07-29, continuing the room sweep)
  `combat_calibration_lab` — the room labelled "P4: Combat Calibration", where a
  new player is taught to fight — stages three enemies: `lab_patrol_dummy`,
  `lab_spitter`, `lab_striker`. None is a catalog character, so none resolves a
  sheet, and all three draw the marked placeholder rectangle with their raw ids
  floating above them.
  ✔ **not a bug, and the code says so on purpose.** §4.10 (Jon's ruling): there is
  NO fallback sheet, because a body borrowing the goblin's art made missing work
  invisible — *"Ambition's own enemies visibly regress until each gets art, which
  is the point: it turns silent debt into visible work."* The mechanism is
  working exactly as specified.
  ▢ **what is worth deciding is WHERE the debt is showing.** It is showing in the
  tutorial room of the opening sequence, which is the highest-traffic square metre
  in the game for the "a stranger runs it" test. Three sprites would change what
  the first fight looks like. Added to
  [awaiting-maintainer-decision.md](awaiting-maintainer-decision.md) as a
  content-priority call, not an engineering one.
  → and their nameplates are the Z′13 family again: `lab_spitter` is an id, shown
  to the player. That is now THREE independent sightings (hub doors, the wake
  room's arrival zone, these enemies), which is the argument for a rule rather
  than a naming pass.
- ✔ **AB6 — the rest of the intro sequence renders.** Swept for completeness:
  `drain_alley` (NPCs, news board, door, signage), `gate_stack_lower` (portal
  ring, Gate Janitor, terminal), `alice_relay`, `under_town_pipes`,
  `intro_raid_corridor`, `first_system_boss`. The only blemish is authored
  DebugLabels overlapping each other (`[manhole…]` over `Bob_route return]`) —
  content placement, not a mechanism.
  ⚠ **and a capture that reads as an EMPTY ROOM may just be a wide one.**
  `pirate_sky_arena` photographed as bare floor and background. It is 2400px wide
  with SIX enemies, all of them past the edge of a 960px frame centred on the
  player's spawn. Pointing the camera at `1200,260` shows a Pirate Raider riding a
  Burning Flying Shark — the ADR 0020 mount pair, both halves resolving their own
  art, no unresolved-sprite warnings.
  → **`capture_scene` takes a COORDINATE as its focus**, not only `player`
  (`capture_scene <room> 1200,260 out.png`). Nothing said so in the sweep notes,
  and it is the difference between "this room is empty" and "I photographed the
  wrong corner of it". Worth knowing before filing an empty room as a defect —
  I nearly did.

- ✔ **AB7 — the AB4 fix broke a possession test, and the test was measuring the
  wrong thing.** (2026-07-29)
  `a_player_can_possess_drive_and_release_an_actor_end_to_end` asserts that the
  vacated avatar does not respond to the drive input, via
  `(player_after.x - player_before.x).abs() < 1.0`. It went red on a body that
  moved **9.7px LEFT** — while the drive input is `move_x(+1.0)`, i.e. RIGHT.
  ✔ **measured before touching it.** An `eprintln!` in the cancel path shows the
  cancel firing at `pos=(957, 879)` — NOT the vacated body at `(909, 897)` — and
  the replacing move carries `start_impulse: None`, so it is not a lunge either.
  Nothing was pushing that body from my change directly.
  → what changed is that enemies now CONNECT. Two fixes landed the same day (this
  row's cancel window, and F0e's carried knockback) turned a fighter that never
  swung into one that does, and the vacated avatar is a live body in a live room.
  It is being hit.
  → the assertion now tests the SIGN rather than the magnitude: the failure mode
  is moving right, because right is where the input points. A body knocked left
  is the guarantee holding, reported as a violation by a proxy that could not
  tell "driven by input" from "struck by something".
  ⚠ **this is a test edit made to accommodate my own change, which is exactly the
  thing to be suspicious of** — so it is written down with the measurement that
  justifies it. `abs()` was never the property the test names in its own message;
  it was a convenient bound taken when nothing in that room could hit anything.

- ✔ **AB8 — Z1's loud-failure requirement was never implemented, and it cost me
  two rooms.** (2026-07-29)
  Z1 said it in the row that specified `--route`: *"it should FAIL LOUDLY on an
  unknown room or route rather than falling back — a capture that silently
  photographs somewhere else is how a blind agent reports the wrong thing with
  confidence."* The `--route` half shipped. The ROOM half did not.
  ⛔ **found by being the blind agent it describes.** Sweeping sandbox rooms, I
  captured `central_hub_basement` and `hall_of_bosses_arena` and got two
  identical images — of the HUB. Then `definitely_not_a_room_xyz` wrote a valid
  960x540 PNG and exited 0. `set_start_by_id` returning false only prints a
  warning and keeps the authored start room, which is right for the game (a stale
  `--start-room` should not stop anyone playing) and fatal for a tool whose job
  is to say what is on screen.
  → `StartRoomMustResolve`, opt-in, inserted by `capture_scene`. An unresolvable
  room id now panics before any frame renders and writes no file.
  → **and the error NAMES THE 72 AVAILABLE IDS**, because the ids this resolves
  against are runtime room ids and NOT the LDtk level identifiers. Reading the
  `.ldtk` and using the level names it lists is exactly how I asked for two rooms
  that do not exist — `central_hub_basement` is a level identifier; the room is
  `central_hub_complex`. An error that only says "no" sends the reader back to
  the wrong file.
  ⚠ **every capture I took before this is suspect in one specific way**: a room
  that silently fell back would have photographed the hub, and the hub is
  recognisable, so the intro sweep's conclusions hold. But that is luck, not
  method — I only noticed because two DIFFERENT ids produced pixel-identical
  output and I happened to tile them side by side.

- ✔ **AB4 — FIXED. A ranged POKE authored no cancel window, so it starved the
  melee finish it was thrown on the way into.** (2026-07-29) The most serious
  thing found today, and the fix is four lines of authored timeline.
  After running `./regen_sprites.sh`, three app tests fail — two real, one a
  poisoned-lock cascade:
  `enemy_attacks_player::a_hostile_enemy_next_to_the_player_attacks_it` and
  `unified_melee::a_hostile_actor_enters_the_same_body_melee_lifecycle`. Both
  spawn a **"Perfect Cellular Automaton"**, and both report the same shape:
  `melee_pressed_frames: 11, swinging_frames: 0` — the brain commits the press
  and the swing never starts.
  ✔ **the same tests PASSED in the 22/22 clean run at `f10ee3898`**, and the only
  functional change since is the regen: it rewrote 22 character sheets that were
  STALE on disk, `perfect_cellular_automaton_spritesheet.png` among them.
  ✔ **and it is NOT either of my renderer changes**, both checked the expensive
  way rather than argued:
  * `demand_actor_character_sheets` — removing it leaves the failure byte-for-byte
    identical (`swinging_frames: 0, min_dist: 34.892403` both ways);
  * the `_fit_text` label guard — the PCA sheet renders to the SAME md5
    (`21562219…`) with and without it, and the PCA is a sheet that HAS a label
    column (`label_width: 100`), so this is the strong version of that check
    rather than the `player_robot` one.
  ⛔ **so the repo's committed RENDERER and its on-disk GENERATED ART disagree
  about gameplay, and the art on disk is the stale one that works.**
  [[feedback-regen-invariant]] says a regen script must work on a fresh clone. A
  fresh clone has no sheets, regenerates them, and gets a build where hostile
  actors cannot swing. That is the "a stranger can run it" case failing.
  ✔ **dated, and it points at in-flight RIG work.** The submodule pin
  (`783b517`, 2026-07-27) already contains a run of rig commits from 2026-07-26 —
  `Animation gui`, `Fix rig pin selection and window controls`, `Improve rig
  editor interactive performance`, `Updage rig animators`, `wip`. The PCA is
  rig-driven (`targets/characters/imperfect_cellular_automaton.py` reads a rig),
  so the sheets on disk predate that work by at least three days and the
  regenerated ones carry it.
  ▢ **next:** the sheets are untracked so there is no old copy to diff. Bisect the
  renderer across that rig range and compare `body_metrics.body_pixel_bbox` and
  `tuning.collision_scale` in the emitted RON. The failure is a swing that never
  starts on a body whose authored metrics moved, so those two fields are the first
  place to look.
  ✔ **root cause CONFIRMED by probe, not by argument.** Bypassing sprite-driven
  body sizing — `let size = ldtk_size;` instead of `sprite_body.map_or(..)` in
  `ActorClusterSeed` — makes the test PASS. So the failure is the body the sprite
  now describes, and nothing else.
  → the numbers: the PCA spawns with a body of **38.1 x 96.3** where the test's
  LDtk placement says 14 x 23, and its authored melee is
  `Swipe { reach_px: 30.0 }`. The brain's attack intent DOES fire
  (`melee_pressed_frames: 11`, and the brain's range floor is 56), the melee
  cooldown is `0.0`, and the sheet still carries all its attack rows
  (`attack_side`, `jab`, `slash`, …) — so it is none of those.
  ⛔ **and the next suspect I wrote down is REFUTED — measured, not argued.** I
  had recorded `move_for_directional_verb`'s `mv.gates.permits(grounded)` as
  what remains, reasoning that a taller body might not be grounded. It is:
  `grounded=Some(true)` on every frame the enemy presses melee. Deleted rather
  than left standing, because a plausible cause that survives in a document is
  what the next reader builds on — the lesson this very ledger records against
  F0e's hitstop story.
  ▢ **so the gate is still unnamed.** Everything cheap is eliminated: grounded is
  true, cooldown is 0, the action set has melee, the press fires, the sheet has
  its attack rows, and the ONLY variable that flips the outcome is body size.
  ✔ **the moveset is fine too** — measured: the body HAS an `ActorMoveset`, with
  verbs `attack, attack_air, attack_air_back, attack_air_down, attack_air_up,
  attack_down, attack_up, ranged, special` and nine moves behind them. So
  `move_for_verb("attack")` has something to find.
  ▢ **the last candidate is the GESTURE.** `trigger_moveset_moves` requires
  `&ResolvedAttackGesture` (not `Option`), and it only triggers when
  `gesture.pressed.is_some()`. `resolve_attack_gestures` in turn needs
  `&AttackGestureState` + `&AttackGestureTuning`, and it arms on an EDGE with
  rearm thresholds — while a brain drives `melee_pressed` as a LEVEL. Eleven
  pressed frames may contain zero rearmed edges.
  ✔ **and one more measurement found it, inside `trigger_moveset_moves` itself.**
  The trigger IS reached, the gesture IS armed (`Some((Forward, Smash))`), the
  body IS grounded — and `playback = Some(("ranged", …))` on every single pressed
  frame. The enemy was playing its RANGED move, continuously, and
  `BodyMelee.swing` stays `None` because a shot is not a swing.
  ⛔ **the defect is that `simple_ranged` authors NO `Cancelable` window**, and a
  move "plays to completion before another starts". So nothing can ever interrupt
  a ranged poke. The fighter brain's own comment states the intent this breaks:
  > Fire WHILE closing, not instead of closing: a ranged poke advances toward the
  > target (throwing the poke on the way in to the melee finish) rather than
  > camping at range.
  A brain that pokes on the way in therefore starves its own melee finish. The
  press arrives, the trigger runs, the gesture is armed, and `cancel_permits`
  says no — silently, every frame.
  → the prefab now authors a `Cancelable` window over its SETTLE (never the
  draw, so a committed shot still fires), permitting `attack` / `smash` /
  `any_attack`, condition `Always` — `OnWhiff` would be wrong because the poke's
  projectile resolves long after the settle, so its hit state says nothing about
  whether closing is right.
  → all three tests green; `ambition_combat` 121 green.
  ⚠ **and the body size was the TRIGGER, not the cause.** The regenerated PCA
  body (38 wide vs the 14 its LDtk placement claimed) tipped the brain from
  melee-range to poke-range, which is what exposed a starvation bug that had been
  sitting in the prefab the whole time. The row spent six measurements hunting
  the art because the art is what CHANGED; the defect was in the timeline all
  along. Worth remembering: "what changed" and "what is wrong" are different
  questions, and I spent a long time answering the first one.
  ⚠ **and a fresh clone is probably GREEN, which is why nobody hit this.** The
  sheets are untracked; a clone with no sheets sizes bodies from LDtk and the
  test passes. The red state needs somebody to have RUN the regen — so this is
  latent for everyone until the day they regenerate, which is exactly the day
  `[[feedback-regen-invariant]]` says must work.
  ⚠ **surfaced rather than fixed, deliberately.** That range is somebody's
  in-progress rig editor work — there is a `wip` commit in it and an uncommitted
  `Update hunny` stash sitting in the submodule right now. Guessing at which rig
  change moved the PCA's metrics, inside a refactor still in flight, is how you
  hand back a merge conflict instead of a fix.
  ⚠ **do NOT "fix" it by editing the tests.** They encode a real capability and
  they were green this morning.

- ✔ **AB2 — CLOSED. "Puppy Slug" rendered as a flat salmon RECTANGLE because
  nothing ever asked for its art.** (2026-07-29, sweeping rooms with the repaired capture tool)
  Four `EnemySpawn`s in `intro_escape_shaft`, and the one on camera is a
  featureless filled rectangle with its name floating above it — the same shape
  as Z′11's blue box, in the intro sequence a stranger plays first.
  ✔ **NOT the art, and not decode latency.** A probe through the facade says
  `try_load_spec_for_character_id("npc_puppy_slug")` is `OK`, target `puppy_slug`
  is among the 578 registered in the baked table, and every referenced sheet file
  exists on disk. A 600-frame warmup renders identically to a 90-frame one, which
  rules out "the sheet had not finished decoding".
  ✔ **not `body_kind` dispatch either.** `puppy_slug` is a `Crawler`, and the
  obvious guess was that no render family handles that. `body_kind` has exactly
  ONE production reader (`autonomous_reconcile`, testing for `Floating`), so it
  is not a render dispatch key.
  ⛔ **what is left is the SPAWN PATH, and the two differ by exactly one thing.**
  The Hall of Characters renders this same cast correctly through `NpcSpawn` with
  an explicit `character_id`. `intro_escape_shaft` uses `EnemySpawn` with
  `name: "Puppy Slug"` and NO character id, which resolves art through
  `catalog.id_for_display_name` — the documented join ("an EnemySpawn named
  'Ai Slop' resolves that character's sheet").
  ✔ **measured, and it is the THIRD MODE.** The `eprintln!` says
  `name="Puppy Slug" sprite_character_id=Some("npc_puppy_slug")` — the
  display-name join works and the art is bound. So the body has a correct sprite
  identity and a render family still does not claim it, which is exactly the
  failure [[feedback-presentation-binding-fails-silently]] says to suspect first.
  ✔ **and the rectangle is IDENTIFIED, by colour.** The measured pixel is
  `(233, 86, 71)`; `feature_color(FeatureVisualKind::Actor, fighting: true)` is
  `srgba(0.93, 0.34, 0.28, 0.96)`. So this body is being drawn by the generic
  FEATURE-VISUAL rectangle — the colour branch — rather than by a sprite.
  ✔ **and the intended path is known**, from an unexpected witness:
  `attach_puppy_slug_deep_dream_overlays` exists specifically for this enemy and
  queries `(&FeatureVisual, &Sprite)`, requiring `sprite.custom_size` and a live
  UV rect. So the design is that this actor's `FeatureVisual` sprite carries the
  sheet's ATLAS, and what shipped is the same sprite carrying a flat colour.
  A whole shader feature is written against a code path this enemy never reaches.
  ✔ **and the ENGINE ANSWERED IT IN ONE LINE, once I stopped tracing and ran it.**
  `upgrade_actor_sprites` already emits a diagnosis for exactly this, once per
  offending name:

  > actor 'Puppy Slug' resolved no sprite and is drawing the placeholder
  > rectangle: **declared as 'npc_puppy_slug' but not materialized — nothing
  > demanded it, so the engine never decoded its sheet**

  It even names "Puppy Slug" in its own hint text. I had spent four greps walking
  the render path by hand when the tool was built to say this.
  → **the gap is a missing sibling.** `demand_worn_character_sheets` watches
  `WornCharacter` — the identity a body PUTS ON. An `EnemySpawn` wears nothing:
  it resolves its character through the display-name join and carries the answer
  on `ActorConfig::sprite_character_id`. So a room of authored enemies declared
  their characters, resolved them correctly, and never asked for the art.
  → `demand_actor_character_sheets` is that sibling. It fixes every EnemySpawn in
  the game that names a catalog character, not just this one.
  → ⚠ **`Added`, not `Changed`.** `ActorConfig` is rebuilt every tick as a
  read-model, so `Changed` would re-request the whole room's cast every frame.
  The identity is decided at construction and does not drift.
  → verified by capture: the slug is a slug, and the warning is gone.
- ✔ **AB3 — the Hall of Characters is FINE, and I nearly filed it as broken.**
  Its cast reads at a glance as fourteen identical placeholder boxes: dark
  rounded bodies, cyan eyes, magenta mouth, under labels as different as "Goblin",
  "Broadside Bess" and "Imperfect Cellular Automaton". I had the row half
  written — "the room whose entire purpose is displaying the cast shows
  placeholders for everyone" — before checking.
  Cropping two of them and differencing pixel-wise shows they are DIFFERENT
  sprites with different proportions; my eye had merged them because this cast
  genuinely shares a visual language. The room is correct.
  ⚠ **worth a row precisely because nothing was wrong.** Two captures earlier I
  read blurry text off a 960x540 image and filed a UI-layout bug that turned out
  to be pixels in a spritesheet. Same failure mode twice in one afternoon:
  looking at a small rendering and reporting the FIRST plausible reading. The
  cheap correction both times was to zoom in and compare rather than to reason
  harder.

- ◐ **AB1 — the first room of the game had never been LOOKED AT, and it had two
  defects.** (2026-07-29, the first thing done with a working room capture)
  Room mode rendered nothing all session, so no room had ever been photographed.
  `intro_wake_room` — the opening scene — renders correctly: the cart, the
  protagonist, the holo-terminal, the Creator, a stasis pod, a crystal.
  ▢ **`wake_room_arrival` is written across the player.** A raw identifier, on
  the first screen a stranger sees. Z′13's exact shape, in the worst possible
  place.
  ✔ **the "overlapping hint labels" were PIXELS IN A SPRITESHEET**, and my first
  reading of them was wrong in an instructive way.
  I read "olographic…da", "t with", "a pulsin…rgy…" off a blurry 960x540 capture
  and filed them as UI labels overlapping and occluding each other. Then I could
  not find any of those strings in the repository — because they are not strings.
  Cropping and upscaling the band made it legible, and grepping the sheet
  generator finished it: the creator-lab prop sheet reserves a **160px annotation
  column** and drew each prop's description into it after truncating at **44
  CHARACTERS**. Forty-four characters of a proportional font is far wider than
  the 150px the column has, so the tail ran past `LABEL_W` into frame 0 — and
  since frames are `alpha_composite`d over that text and prop art is mostly
  transparent, the overflow showed THROUGH the sprite.
  → so the game's opening room drew its Neural Console with the words
  "…holographic … la…" baked across it, and the Genesis Vat and Power Core the
  same. It looked exactly like a UI layer with a z-order bug. It was the art.
  → `_fit` measures with `textlength` and returns the longest prefix plus an
  ellipsis that still fits; all three label lines go through it. Verified by
  re-capturing: only the legitimate "Creator" nameplate remains.
  → the fix is in the `ambition_sprite2d_renderer` SUBMODULE (`62e89a2`); the
  sheets themselves are generated and untracked, so there is no asset to commit.
  ✔ **and the same shape is guarded everywhere else, inertly.** The shared
  grid-sheet builder draws an animation name and a frame count into its label
  column with NO limit at all, so a long rename is one commit away from the same
  artifact on any sheet. Clipped by measurement there too.
  ⚠ **verified inert rather than assumed inert:** `player_robot` renders
  byte-identical with and without the guard (same md5 across a stash/unstash
  pair). That mattered, because a full regen changed 22 sheets and the obvious
  reading was "my guard did that". It did not — those sheets were stale on disk
  relative to the current generator, and the two-run comparison is what
  separated the two explanations.
  ⚠ **and the probe caught a real hazard in the guard itself.** A sheet
  declaring NO column (`label_width == 0`) has always drawn its labels straight
  over frame 0; clipping to a zero-width column would have DELETED that text
  rather than clipped it. No column now means no clipping. A guard that silently
  removes content is worse than the overflow it was written for.
  ⚠ **I nearly built the wrong fix.** "Labels overlap" pointed at label layout —
  z-order, de-confliction, show-only-the-nearest. Every one of those would have
  been real work on the wrong file. What stopped it was that the strings were not
  in the repo, and taking that seriously instead of assuming I had misread.
  → Z′13 is REFRAMED by counting: **130 of 151 `LoadingZone`s across every
  authored world carry an id-shaped name.** It was filed as "pick three strings".
  At 130 it is a rule, and the options are genuinely different products
  (author / derive from the destination room / show nothing). Moved to
  [awaiting-maintainer-decision.md](awaiting-maintainer-decision.md) with the
  count and a recommendation.
  ⚠ **and this is the argument for the capture tool in one row.** The tool was
  broken, so nobody looked, so the opening room shipped with an identifier
  written over the protagonist. Every visible defect this session came from
  looking at a picture; none came from the suite.

- ▢ **Z′13 — the doors display their raw ids, and it is CONTENT, not debug.**
  `military_tower_door`, `hall_of_bosses_door`, `pirate_cove_door` survive a
  `--dev-overlays`-off capture, because a door nameplate renders `zone.name` and
  the authored LDtk zones set `name` to the id string.
  I assumed these were debug nameplates and said so; they are not.
  → **this is Jon's call, not mine.** [[feedback-entity-id-matches-label]] says the
  fix for label/id drift is to rename the IDS to match the LABEL, do it as its own
  commit across LDtk + code + tests + docs, and *surface it rather than silently
  introducing a third name*. Picking display names for three doors is authoring.
  The engine side is already correct: `DoorNameplateSource` carries an `id` and a
  `label` separately, so nothing needs to change for a real label to appear.
- ✔ **Z′14 — CLOSED. ROOM-mode capture pointed at an asset directory that cannot exist.**
  `capture_scene <ROOM_ID> <focus>` — the usage in the tool's own header — renders
  a background grid, the room's hint text, and nothing else. No doors, no
  platforms, no player. It writes a valid PNG and exits 0.
  ✔ **NOT a regression**: identical with today's `HostGameplayPresentationPlugin`
  addition removed, and identical at 60 and 200 warmup frames. It predates this
  session. `--route ambition_gameplay` renders the SAME content correctly, which
  is why nobody noticed — the shipped surfaces are routes.
  ⛔ **I do not know the cause, and three guesses were all wrong.** Recorded
  because a wrong diagnosis in a ledger is worse than none:
  1. "no gameplay session, so nothing session-scoped spawns" — but `RoomVisual`
     entities exist;
  2. "no room visuals" — the hint label carries `RoomVisual`, so that predicate
     passes while the shot is empty;
  3. "no player body" — a `PrimaryPlayerOnly` body EXISTS.
  So the entities are there and are either not drawn or not in frame. The camera
  is the obvious next suspect, and it has not been checked.
  ⚠ **I wrote a readiness guard twice and deleted it both times**, because each
  predicate passed against the broken output. A guard that cannot go red on the
  defect it names is noise in the codebase; the third attempt would have been
  guessing again.
  ✔ **INSTRUMENTED, and it eliminated four hypotheses including two of my own
  from the paragraph above.** Measured in a `central_hub_main` capture:
  * the staged world is correct — `"Ambition: central hub complex"`, 1900x2004,
    `active_room="central_hub_complex"`;
  * the player body is at `(950, 904)`, dead centre of it;
  * **138 `RoomVisual` entities exist**;
  * the MainCamera write lands on the ACTIVE order-0 camera;
  * and its position `(0, 120.5)` is CORRECT — `x = 950 - 1900/2 = 0`,
    `y = 2004/2 - 904 = 98`, i.e. the player converted into Bevy's centre-origin
    space. My "the camera is looking 1300 units away" reading was wrong too.
  ⛔ **so the entities exist, the camera is right, and nothing draws.** The one
  concrete absence found: `PlayerVisual` never matches, so there is no render-side
  player at all. That is also why `player` focus silently fell back — the focus
  query filtered on `PlayerVisual` while the kinematics live on the SIM body.
  ✔ the focus query is fixed (it reads the sim body now, which is correct on its
  own merits regardless of this defect).
  ✔ **that measurement is done too, and it was NOT a coordinate-space problem.**
  Room visuals sit at sensible positions around the camera — `(935, 138)`,
  `(-935, 138)`, `(20, 590)` against a camera at `(0, 120.5)` — and the
  orthographic scale is a normal `0.833`. So position and zoom are both right.
  What IS wrong: `ViewVisibility` is `false`. And `RoomVisual` turns out to mark
  entities that have **no `Visibility` component at all**, so it is not a reliable
  "this is art" marker and any guard built on it counts the wrong population — the
  reason the second readiness guard passed.
  ▢ **remaining suspect, and it is a KNOWN one:**
  [[feedback-presentation-binding-fails-silently]] lists three distinct failures
  and says to suspect *"no render family claims it"* FIRST. That is exactly the
  shape here: entities exist, are positioned, are in frame, and nothing draws them.
  ✔ **two more measurements, and the framing is EXONERATED.** The camera centres
  on the player at `(950, 904)` and the room's authored spawn is `(950, 883)` —
  the player is standing on its spawn and the camera is on the player. Nothing
  about position, zoom or focus is wrong.
  And the culling is correct too: at scale `0.833` on a 960x540 target the view is
  ±400 x ±225, so the sampled visuals at x=935, x=-935 and y=590 are genuinely
  outside it. They were an unrepresentative sample of 138.
  ⛔ **the remaining difference is WHICH ENTITIES EXIST.** Two of three sampled
  `RoomVisual`s had no `Visibility` component at all, meaning no `Sprite` —
  logical entities, not art. Route mode stages the SAME room at the SAME spawn and
  renders doors, platforms and the player; room mode appears to spawn the logical
  half and little of the sprite half. Room mode builds its own app around
  `SandboxPresentationPlugin` while route mode goes through `build_visible_app`.
  ✔ **FOUND, and it was one character.** `capture_scene` carried its OWN copy of
  the asset-root rule, and the copy read `crates/ambition::actors/assets` — a
  `::` where the crate name has a `_`. No such directory can exist,
  `canonicalize` failed every single time, and the fallback pointed the room
  composition at the workspace-root `assets/` tree, which holds IPFS metadata and
  none of the actor sprites, shaders or sounds. A valid PNG of a room whose art
  never resolved, exit 0 (GPT 5.6, 2026-07-29).
  → the tool now calls `ambition_asset_manager::actors_desktop_asset_root()`, the
  shared helper that exists *because* a demo rendering nothing standalone was
  this exact divergence once before. Route mode goes through the visible app and
  its correct root, which is precisely why `--route` looked fine while rooms did
  not.
  → verified by capture: the hub renders doors, platforms, the player, two NPCs,
  the hint text and the parallax background.
  ⚠ **what the six measurements were worth, honestly.** They eliminated the
  camera, the framing, the culling, the entity population and three wrong
  hypotheses, and left the row at "the entities exist and the sprite half does
  not" — which is a correct description of a missing asset root and did not find
  it. A reviewer reading the file found it in one pass. Measurement narrowed the
  question; it took READING THE PATH to answer it, and I never read the path
  because it was in a function I had not changed.
  ⚠ **stopping for real.** I said six measurements and took eight; the extra two
  were worth it (they eliminated the camera entirely) but the discipline point
  stands. This is a verification-TOOL gap — `--route` renders every shipped surface
  correctly — and it has now consumed more of this session than any game defect
  did.
  ⚠ five wrong hypotheses on this one defect, every one killed by a print rather
  than by more reasoning. The pattern is now unambiguous enough to state as a rule:
  on a rendering question, measure FIRST.
- ▢ **Z′15 — one UNREPRODUCED `cargo test --workspace` failure, cause discarded.**
  2026-07-29: the suite reported 21/22 with `workspace (default features)` failing
  at 162.6s. It passes standalone (exit 0), the three suite runs immediately before
  it were 22/22 on the same code, and the re-run right after was 22/22 in 119s.
  ⛔ **the cause is unknowable because I threw the evidence away.**
  `scripts/run_tests.py` runs each job with `subprocess.run(argv)` and does NOT
  capture output, so every panic message and compiler error streams through the
  runner — and I had been piping the suite through `tail -30` all session. The
  summary survived; the reason did not.
  ✔ ruled out: job-vs-job contention (jobs run SEQUENTIALLY), disk (55%), memory
  (39 GB free), and the stale-rlib link failure (that one names `mold:` and this
  did not reach the log).
  → **always** `./run_tests.sh > <scratchpad>/suite.log 2>&1` and grep the log.
  If this recurs the log will name it; recording the row so a second sighting is
  recognised as a pattern rather than dismissed as another one-off.

## AC. GPT-5.6 review round 5 (2026-07-29) — "a diagnostic is not a fix"

Three findings, all of the same shape: a value the campaign had made
authoritative was still being *consulted* by only some of its consumers, or was
being *reported* about instead of *enforced*. The reviewer's own summary line is
the useful one — **"a diagnostic without behavioral rejection is insufficient."**

- ✔ **AC1 — a host-code character could author a `ranged` verb, and BOTH owners
  fired.** (2026-07-29)
  `PreparedKit::HostCode` carries `authored_moveset` so a character can take the
  host's capabilities and bring its own timelines. If that moveset binds `ranged`,
  the press is owned twice: by the charge-projectile path the kit installs, and by
  the moveset verb.
  ⛔ **and it is two projectiles, not one that quietly does nothing.**
  `default_player_action_set` always sets `ranged: Some(bolt)`, and
  `simple_ranged`'s doc says outright that *"the move carries NO projectile spec —
  the fire event samples the owner's live `ActionSet.ranged`"*. So the authored
  move has something to throw and throws it, beside the charge shot.
  ⛔ **the previous pass's fix was a LOG LINE.** It detected the contradiction at
  finalization, printed it at error level, and published the kit unchanged. That
  named the bug for anyone reading stderr and changed nothing about what reached a
  body — which is the exact failure mode the review's summary line describes, and
  I wrote it one review earlier while believing it was the fix.
  → `revoke_host_owned_ranged` strips the verb bindings at finalization, so the
  contradiction cannot reach runtime and `RangedExecution` is the sole authority.
  → **the whole verb FAMILY, not the base.** `directional_verb_chain` resolves a
  press through `ranged_air_forward` → `ranged_forward` → `ranged_air` → `ranged`,
  so watching only `"ranged"` would have left the same double-fire in the air and
  nowhere else — invisible until somebody shot while jumping.
  → the MOVES survive; only the bindings go. A timeline nothing presses is inert,
  and deleting authored content on a reachability argument is the more expensive
  mistake. It costs an over-declared cue in the load inventory, which is cheap and
  honest about what the author wrote.
  ⚠ this does NOT touch the *incomplete* kit — a moveset binding `ranged` with no
  payload on the action set. That one is still reported and not repaired, and
  deliberately: a missing projectile is an authoring error whose fix is authoring,
  and silently dropping the verb would hide it. Different failure, different
  remedy.
  ⚠ **and it changes NOTHING about shipped content today**, which is worth saying
  plainly rather than letting a closed row imply a repaired game. Checked, not
  assumed: the five registered characters are `mary_o`, `mary_o_tall`, `sanic`,
  `super_sanic`, `pocket_runner` and the two duelists. The first five author a
  sheet and nothing else, so their `authored_moveset` is `None` and the strip
  never runs; the duelists author an action set (the `Authored` arm) and bind only
  `attack*` verbs. This is a door closed on a configuration the public API permits
  and no current content reaches — which is the right time to close one, and not
  the same claim as "a bug players were hitting is fixed".

- ✔ **AC2 — `ActiveMatch` held live `Entity` values in a resource written from
  inside the rollback schedule.** (2026-07-29)
  Seating runs on `sim`, which under a session IS `GgrsSchedule`. `ActiveMatch` is
  not rollback-registered, so a rewind across the activation tick restores the
  fighters and leaves the resource naming them — or naming entities that no longer
  exist.
  → **the fix was to stop snapshotting.** The review's rule is *"do not snapshot
  raw entity references without a complete remapping and reconstruction
  contract"*, and the cheap reading of it is that the binding already lives
  somewhere better: every fighter wears `MatchSeat(i)`. `match_participants`
  derives the cast in seat order from the world; `ActiveMatch` keeps a seat COUNT
  and a topology generation, both plain data. It rewinds because the bodies do.
  ⚠ **there was exactly one production reader of the entity list, and it was a
  `warn_once!` counting them.** The list had been carrying a rollback hazard to
  serve a log line. Worth remembering before adding a convenience field to a
  resource: ask who reads it, not whether it would be useful.
  ▢ **the LIFECYCLE half is still open**, and it is the half the review calls
  preferred. Activation still happens inside the rollback schedule, so a rewind
  across it leaves a non-rollback latch saying "live" while the fighters are
  pre-activation. The failure is now BOUNDED — a count cannot dangle, and the
  disagreement is detectable by comparing `seats()` against `match_participants()`
  — but bounded is not closed. The fix remains route one: freeze the topology,
  prepare every fighter, construct the match, publish, then start the session.

- ✔ **AC3 — the same character was a different physical object depending on how
  it got its body.** (2026-07-29)

  | | max health | mass | `Explicit` box |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | spawned match fighter | the definition | the definition | yes |
  | adopted match fighter | the definition | the definition | yes |
  | worn exploration player | the CATALOG row | never set | no |

  ⛔ **and the obvious fix would have nerfed the entire cast to 1 HP.**
  `Vitals::default()` was `max_health: 1`, which is indistinguishable from an
  authored one-hit glass cannon — so applying prepared vitals universally would
  have handed a one-hit pool to every character that never thought about health.
  That is *why* only seating read it: the value could not be applied anywhere
  else without breaking something.
  → `Vitals` fields are now `Option`, carrying the meaning every other kit field
  on a definition carries: `None` is *the author said nothing*. Making the absence
  expressible is what let one applier serve every path.
  → and health FOLDS at the barrier, definition first then the catalog row — which
  has carried `max_health: Option<i32>` with exactly this meaning since it was
  added. Two authorities that had never met.
  → `PhysicalBaseline` resolves the numbers once; `seat_character`, match
  adoption, `session::setup` and the worn-character derive all read it.
  ⚠ **`BaselineBoundary` is two variants because the boundaries genuinely
  differ.** Construction opens at FULL health; REPLACEMENT clamps the current
  value under the new maximum and does not refill, because wearing a character
  mid-round must not be a free heal.
  ⚠ **geometry is narrowed rather than universalised, and the reason is Jon's
  rule.** `Replacement` does not resize: re-resolving a body's pose against the
  world is the transit seam's job (ADR 0024), the re-wear system has nowhere to
  transit to, and nothing may shove a body out of geometry. A silhouette that must
  follow a character onto every body is `SpriteAuthored`, whose per-pose
  projection already reaches every path. `Explicit` is a construction-time size
  and now says so — which is the review's own "narrow the API instead of
  presenting it as universal".
  ⚠ **the mass write became CONDITIONAL, which is a fix to the previous fix.**
  Writing `Mass(prepared.vitals.mass)` into the seating bundle unconditionally
  meant a character that authored no mass overwrote its own roster archetype's
  with the ambient 1.0.
  ⚠ threading the registry into `session::setup` pushed the caller to 17 system
  params, one over Bevy's limit. Grouped as `CharacterAuthorities` — catalog,
  prepared registry, sheets, roster — which is four questions about one subject
  and should have been one parameter regardless.

- ✔ **AC4 — `--character` had NEVER worked, for any id, including the one in its
  own usage example.** (2026-07-29, found by Jon's versioning ask)
  `capture_scene`'s header documents
  `--character npc_pirate_admiral`; every non-default id panicked the boot with
  `ContentDiagnostic { section: "provider.defaults" }`.
  ⛔ **two different facts were wearing one check.** Preparation required the
  effective starting character to EQUAL the provider's authored default:
  `if effective_character != authored.starting_character { Err(..) }`. The
  provider's DEFAULT is an authoring fact worth validating; which character a
  session SELECTED is a runtime choice, and the entire point of a playable cast
  is that it has members other than the first one. Collapsing them made the
  cast unreachable from direct entry.
  → the check now validates what actually matters: the default AND the selection
  each RESOLVE against the assembled catalog. A selection naming nothing is
  still fatal — that is the failure the check was reaching for.
  ⚠ the SHELL path was fine, because it skips `publish_direct_prepared_session_root`
  entirely. So character-select worked in the launcher and every direct-entry
  consumer — the capture tool, `run_game.sh` flags, any headless harness — could
  only ever spawn the default. A feature that works in one of two hosts is the
  recurring shape in this repo (`--dev-overlays` in route-but-not-room mode, the
  asset root, the headless surface insert).
  ⚠ **found by trying to take the picture**, not by reading the check. The ask
  was "let me play as an older version of the robot"; the roster edit was two
  lines and passed its unit test, and the tool still could not photograph it.

- ✔ **AC5 — the player robot's lineage is IN the playable cast.** (2026-07-29)
  The catalog has always told this story — v2's row says `robot` is v0, that
  there is no v1 ("a joke, not a gap"), and that Ambition *"wants old versions
  of yourself to be things you can meet, talk to, and fight"*. Two of the three
  could be met and fought and neither could be WORN.
  → the current body is `player_robot_v3` (art renamed across the submodule and
  the main repo), and `robot` + `npc_player_robot_v2` joined `PLAYABLE_ROSTER`,
  so swapping the player between incarnations is a selection rather than a
  content edit.
  → **verified by capture, not by the roster test**: all three worn in
  `central_hub_complex`, pixel-differenced against each other and against
  `npc_pirate_admiral` as a control. Four distinct bodies — v0's chunky visor,
  v2's smaller build, v3's SVG rig with the magenta antenna.
  ✔ **the SHARED COMPOSITION, done the way the repo already says to.**
  `ambition_content::player_robot_lineage` is one generator over an
  `Incarnation` struct (id, display name, sheet, what it replaces); adding v4 is
  a struct literal. The rule it follows is `Lineage`'s own: *"two independent,
  fully-resolved products with distinct stable ids, emitted by one generator
  from shared source"* — no patch layer, no override precedence — the same shape
  `versus_fighters::DuelistNumbers` uses for the duelists.
  ⚠ `Lineage::derived_from` is PROVENANCE, not an inheritance edge, and the
  module says so twice because it is the one way this design can rot: nothing
  resolves through it and no field of v3 comes from v2.
  ⚠ the KIT is deliberately NOT authored on the definitions. Each incarnation's
  catalog row already states what it can do and preparation folds it at the
  barrier; authoring it again would be two declarations of one fact, which is
  the split the whole character-authority campaign exists to remove.
  ▢ and the generic `player` catalog row should retire in favour of
  `player_robot_v3`: ~10 production sites plus tests. `attack_hitbox.rs` carries
  `PLAYER_FILE_ROOT` and `PLAYER_CHARACTER_ID` as a PAIR precisely because the
  sheet root and the character id disagreed; retiring the row collapses them,
  which is evidence the versioned naming is the right shape.

- ▢ **AC6 — `capture_scene` is NOT deterministic, so it is a weaker oracle than
  every conclusion drawn from it assumes.** (2026-07-29)
  Two runs of the same binary with identical arguments differ by ~132 px; the
  same room captured before and after an unrelated change differed by 800–1450.
  I nearly filed that larger number as a rendering regression.
  ✔ **it is idle-ANIMATION PHASE**, established by tiling before/after
  side-by-side and looking: same silhouette, same size, same art, arms in a
  different part of the bob. Not a resize, not a re-body.
  ⛔ **but the tool's own contract is broken by it.** `--warmup N` exists to
  reach a fixed state, and it does not: the animation clock advances by wall
  time somewhere in the capture path, so "N frames in" is not a pose. Every
  pixel-difference conclusion this session rests on the differences being much
  larger than the noise floor — which held each time, and was luck rather than
  method.
  → the fix is to drive the capture's warmup on a FIXED tick (the fixed-tick
  fixture already exists — `ManualDuration`), so N frames means one pose. Then a
  zero-diff is evidence instead of a coincidence, and the sweep can compare a
  room against its own baseline rather than against a recognisable landmark.

- ✔ **AC7 — the generic `player` catalog id is RETIRED; the protagonist is
  `player_robot_v3`.** (2026-07-29, Jon: *"I don't want a generic notion of
  it"*)
  `player` was the one id in the lineage that meant "whichever incarnation is
  current", which is the generic notion the versioning exists to remove. Every
  incarnation is now named for which one it IS: `robot` (v0),
  `player_robot_v2` (the `npc_` prefix went with it — that prefix meant "you can
  meet this one but not be it", and that stopped being true when it joined the
  playable cast), and `player_robot_v3`.
  → **the collapse is the evidence the naming was right.** `attack_hitbox.rs`
  carried `PLAYER_FILE_ROOT` and `PLAYER_CHARACTER_ID` as a PAIR, existing only
  because the sheet was `player_robot` and the character was `player` — two
  names for one thing. They are the same string now. The LDtk-tools codegen
  carried the same split as an ALIAS (`"player_robot" -> "player"`); it is an
  identity and is gone.
  ⚠ display name follows the id: the protagonist reads "Player Robot v3", beside
  v2's "Player Robot v2" and v0's "Robot". Consistent with
  [[feedback-entity-id-matches-label]] and with three pedestals in the Hall
  introducing themselves — but it IS a player-visible string, so it is called
  out rather than buried.
  ⚠ `"player"` survives in ~10 places that are NOT the catalog id —
  `ActorFaction::Player` labels, `tags: ["player"]`, `Brain::Player`, a
  trace-file JSON key, `hall_player` dialogue. Each was classified by hand; there
  is no regex over the word, because a regex over `"player"` in this repo is how
  a rename becomes an outage.
  → verified by capture: v0, v2, v3 and the DEFAULT (no `--character`) all
  render, and the default is the same body as explicit v3.

- ✔ **AC8 — `ProjectedCharacterKit` was never rollback state, and registering the
  protagonist's incarnations is what made anyone notice.** (2026-07-29)
  The projection early-exits when its memo agrees with the body's worn id and the
  cast generation, and the memo also records what it GRANTED so it can retract
  exactly that. Unregistered, a rewind restores an earlier `WornCharacter` while
  the memo still claims the abandoned future's id: the projection skips, and the
  body resimulates wearing hurtboxes, movement tuning and a sprite-posed body its
  identity no longer asks for. `granted` makes it worse than a stale read —
