  retraction is driven from it, so the wrong record retracts the wrong facts.
  ⚠ **it was ALWAYS unregistered.** Nothing caught it because no tested room had
  a body carrying one: the component served SEATED fighters, and the coverage
  gate's rooms have none. Putting the protagonist's own incarnations in the cast
  landed it on the PLAYER, so it appeared in every room at once and three gates
  went red together. The component did not become dangerous that day, it became
  VISIBLE — which is the argument for a coverage gate that sweeps a live room
  rather than a list somebody maintains.
  ⚠ **third time for this exact shape**, after `IdentityKit` and
  `PersonaBaseline` (whose registration comment describes the identical hazard,
  two entries up in the same function). A derive that memoises "already applied"
  is rollback state, always, and the memo is easier to forget than the value it
  guards. Registered PROBED over id + generation + grant set, because the failure
  is silent by construction: it does not corrupt a number, it makes a derive skip.

- ◐ **AC6 — the capture clock is pinned in BOTH builders; the noise floor is not
  gone and its cause is NOT established.** (2026-07-29)
  `capture_scene` advances with `ScheduleRunnerPlugin::run_loop(ZERO)` and Bevy's
  default clock, so a frame of warmup was "however long that frame took" and
  `--warmup 60` bought sixty frames of whatever the CPU managed.
  → `TimeUpdateStrategy::ManualDuration(1/SIM_TICK_HZ)` in room mode AND route
  mode. A warmup frame is a sim tick now, which is right on its own merits: the
  flag's contract is that N means a fixed amount of elapsed world.
  ⛔ **but it did not make the tool deterministic, and saying otherwise would be
  the exact overclaim this row exists to prevent.** Three runs after pinning:
  69, 173 and 226 differing pixels — indistinguishable from the ~132 measured
  before. Whatever the remaining source is (software-rasterizer float, async
  asset arrival changing which frame the warmup starts from), pinning the clock
  did not remove it, and I have not localised it: the differing pixels are sparse
  across the whole frame rather than on the body.
  ⚠ so every pixel-diff conclusion in this session still rests on signal being
  6–11× the noise, which it was each time. That is a real margin, not a proof.

- ⊘ **AC9 — RETRACTED: the hub does NOT render nameplates by two different
  conventions.** (2026-07-29)
  I read `military_tower_door` with underscores and `tech bros door` with spaces
  in the same frame, confirmed both are authored `activation: Door`, found
  "tech bros door" is not a literal anywhere in the repo, and had a two-code-path
  theory half-written.
  ⛔ **it was my own resampling.** I upscaled with `Image.LANCZOS`, which smooths;
  at that size it erased the underscores on the brighter plate. Re-cropped with
  `NEAREST` at 8× and the underscores are there — dim, short, and present. Both
  plates render `zone.name` verbatim. Z′13 stands exactly as written.
  ⚠ this is [[reference-text-in-a-capture-may-be-pixels]] catching me a second
  time, and the tell that saved it is the same one: **the string was not in the
  repository.** Last time that meant "it is baked into a sheet"; this time it
  meant "you are not reading what you think you are reading". Either way, a
  rendered string with no source is a measurement problem before it is a code
  problem. ⚠ **never conclude anything about small text from a smoothed upscale**
  — `NEAREST` or don't look.

- ✔ **AC10 — a registered character can bring its own VOICE, so nobody stands
  mute on a Hall pedestal.** (2026-07-29, Jon: *"in the hall there are
  characters that don't have any lines"*)
  Measured before fixing: **4 of 144 Hall pedestals** stage a character with no
  catalog row at all — `sanic`, `super_sanic`, `mary_o`, `mary_o_tall`. The
  ambient ticker skips whoever `npc_ambient_bark_line` answers `None` for, so
  "registered-only" and "silent in the gallery" were the same state.
  ⛔ **the mechanism already existed and could not reach them.**
  `CharacterCatalogEntry::fallback_dialogue` says exactly the right thing —
  *"a character arrives from the sprite pipeline with a voice long before anyone
  writes four separate pools for it … the fallback exists so a newly authored
  character is never mute"* — and it is a field on a CATALOG ROW. Every
  character another game registers has no row, so the promise did not cover the
  population it was written for.
  → `CharacterDefinition::voice` (+ `with_voice`), carried through preparation
  onto the published value, and consulted by the bark authority when the catalog
  answers nothing.
  ⚠ **precedence, lowest LAST**: a yarn node wins, then the catalog row's
  situation pool, then its `fallback_dialogue`, then the definition's voice. A
  floor, not an override — pinned by a test that a catalog `Idle` pool still
  beats a definition that also brought lines.
  ⚠ the seam test opens with a VACUITY CHECK: the same call with no registry
  returns `None`. Without it the test would pass against a stub that always had
  a line, which is the shape of guard that has gone green-and-useless twice this
  session.
  → Sanic, Super Sanic, Mary-O and Mary-O (Tall) now have three lines each, and
  the three player-robot incarnations speak about their own place in the
  sequence — which is the joke of preserving them: the Hall is a room full of
  drafts of you.
  ✔ **and the repo-wide guard, which immediately found SIX MORE.**
  `every_composed_character_can_say_at_least_one_line` walks the composed cast —
  the shipped host's catalog plus every provider's registrations — and went red
  on `ai_slop`, `solid_snake`, `sanic_badnik`, `arena_duelist_close`,
  `arena_duelist_long` and `pocket_runner`. All voiced; ten silent characters in
  total, where hand-inspection had found four.
  ⚠ **the guard's own first version was WRONG in the direction that matters.**
  It read `PreparedCharacterRegistry` off a freshly-built App and reported three
  characters mute that had voices — because `register_character` QUEUES a
  definition and the registry is published once, whole, at `Plugin::finish`. A
  test that reads a partial cast reports false positives today and would report a
  genuinely silent character as fine tomorrow. It calls `finalize` first now.
  ⚠ it also carries a population floor (`ids.len() > 50`), because a sweep whose
  population silently became empty is a guard that passes forever.
